--- Thread 2 (ID: PRRT_kwDOQ6DXvM511Skg) ---
Author: coderabbitai
File: packages/sdk/src/client.ts:360
Comment ID (db): 2968390773
Body: _⚠️ Potential issue_ | _🟠 Major_

**Serialize same-target mutations to avoid lost updates.**

These mutators all snapshot `children`/`binState`, publish a full replacement, then write that snapshot back locally. If two calls hit the same folder or bin concurrently, both can derive from the same baseline and the later stale-sequence retry still republishes its stale view, dropping the earlier mutation. A keyed async mutex/queue per folder IPNS name (and one for bin state) would make the client sa

--- Thread 3 (ID: PRRT_kwDOQ6DXvM511Ski) ---
Author: coderabbitai
File: packages/sdk/src/client.ts:628
Comment ID (db): 2968390775
Body: _⚠️ Potential issue_ | _🟠 Major_

**Guarantee `uploadResult.fileKey` cleanup across the whole post-upload path.**

Once `sdkCore.uploadFile()` returns, any exception from the folder publish path or the awaited share re-wrap exits before `clearBytes(uploadResult.fileKey)` runs. That leaves the plaintext AES key resident after a failed call, and a best-effort share sync can make an already-persisted upload look failed to the caller.

<details>
<summary>🔧 Suggested fix</summary>

       con

--- Thread 4 (ID: PRRT_kwDOQ6DXvM511Skk) ---
Author: coderabbitai
File: packages/sdk/src/client.ts:947
Comment ID (db): 2968390777
Body: _⚠️ Potential issue_ | _🟠 Major_

**Guard the lifecycle hooks so observer code can't change SDK semantics.**

`onOperationStart` runs before the `try`, and `onOperationEnd` / `onError` are invoked inline on the success/error path. A throwing consumer callback can block the underlying operation, turn a successful call into a rejection, or mask the original error.

<details>
<summary>🔧 Suggested fix</summary>

```diff
+  private notifySafely(fn: () => void): void {
+    try {
+      fn();
+    } c

Author: coderabbitai
File: tests/e2e/tests/sharing-workflow.spec.ts:157
Comment ID (db): 2968390778
Body: _⚠️ Potential issue_ | _🟠 Major_

**Increase the timeout for the three-account bootstrap.**

Line 153 caps this test at 180s, but Lines 155-157 create three accounts sequentially and each helper can spend up to 90s in `loginViaWallet()` plus another 30s waiting for the vault UI. On a slow devnet, Playwright can abort the test before the third login finishes even though each individual step is still within its own timeout.

<details>
<summary>⏱️ Suggested fix</summary>

```diff
-    test.setTimeo

--- Thread 6 (ID: PRRT_kwDOQ6DXvM511Skn) ---
Author: coderabbitai
File: tests/e2e/utils/multi-account-wallet.ts:105
Comment ID (db): 2968390781
Body: _⚠️ Potential issue_ | _🟠 Major_

**Make wallet account bootstrap fail-fast and leak-free.**

If anything after Line 76 throws, `createWalletTestAccount()` loses the freshly created `BrowserContext`. And if a later iteration fails on Lines 117-118, the already-created accounts are lost too. One transient login/auth-state failure can therefore leave orphaned contexts behind and poison later E2E runs.

<details>
<summary>♻️ Suggested fix</summary>

```diff
 export async function createWalletTestAc
```

     1→/**
     2→ * @cipherbox/crypto
     3→ *
     4→ * Pure cryptographic primitives and key derivation for CipherBox.
     5→ * Provides AES-256-GCM/CTR encryption, ECIES key wrapping, Ed25519 signing,
     6→ * and key hierarchy management.
     7→ *
     8→ * Security principles:
     9→ * - All operations use Web Crypto API or audited libraries (@noble/*, eciesjs)
    10→ * - Error messages are generic to prevent oracle attacks
    11→ * - Keys are Uint8Array - never convert to/from strings for sensitive data
    12→ * - Private keys exist in memory only - never persisted to storage
    13→ *
    14→ * @example
    15→ * ```typescript
    16→ * import {
    17→ *   generateFileKey,
    18→ *   generateIv,
    19→ *   encryptAesGcm,
    20→ *   decryptAesGcm,
    21→ *   wrapKey,
    22→ *   unwrapKey
    23→ * } from '@cipherbox/crypto';
    24→ *
    25→ * // Encrypt file content
    26→ * const fileKey = generateFileKey();
    27→ * const iv = generateIv();
    28→ * const ciphertext = await encryptAesGcm(plaintext, fileKey, iv);
    29→ *
    30→ * // Wrap file key with user's public key
    31→ * const wrappedKey = await wrapKey(fileKey, vaultKey.publicKey);
    32→ *
    33→ * // Unwrap and decrypt
    34→ * const unwrappedKey = await unwrapKey(wrappedKey, vaultKey.privateKey);
    35→ * const decrypted = await decryptAesGcm(ciphertext, unwrappedKey, iv);
    36→ * ```
    37→ */
    38→
    39→export const CRYPTO_VERSION = '0.2.0';
    40→
    41→// Vault IPNS key derivation (only derive-ipns remains in crypto)
    42→export { deriveVaultIpnsKeypair } from './vault';
    43→
    44→// Key hierarchy and derivation
    45→export { deriveKey, deriveContextKey, generateFolderKey, type DeriveKeyParams } from './keys';
    46→
    47→// AES-256-GCM symmetric encryption
    48→export { encryptAesGcm, decryptAesGcm, sealAesGcm, unsealAesGcm } from './aes';
    49→
    50→// AES-256-CTR streaming encryption (random-access decryption for media)
    51→export { encryptAesCtr, decryptAesCtr, decryptAesCtrRange } from './aes';
    52→
    53→// ECIES secp256k1 key wrapping
    54→export { wrapKey, unwrapKey, reWrapKey } from './ecies';
    55→
    56→// Ed25519 signing for IPNS
    57→export { generateEd25519Keypair, type Ed25519Keypair } from './ed25519';
    58→export { signEd25519, verifyEd25519 } from './ed25519/sign';
    59→
    60→// IPNS name derivation (kept in crypto as a pure crypto utility)
    61→export { deriveIpnsName } from './ipns/derive-name';
    62→
    63→// Device identity (per-device Ed25519 keypair)
    64→export { generateDeviceKeypair, deriveDeviceId, type DeviceKeypair } from './device';
    65→
    66→// Utility functions (only safe public utilities)
    67→export {
    68→  hexToBytes,
    69→  bytesToHex,
    70→  concatBytes,
    71→  clearBytes,
    72→  clearAll,
    73→  generateRandomBytes,
    74→  generateFileKey,
    75→  generateIv,
    76→  generateCtrIv,
    77→} from './utils';
    78→
    79→// Types

    81→

   372→  async renameItem(folderIpnsName: string, childId: string, newName: string): Promise<void> {
   373→    return this.withOperation('renameItem', async () => {
   374→      const folder = this.folderTree.get(folderIpnsName);
   375→      if (!folder) throw new Error('Folder not loaded');

   386→        children: updatedChildren,

   388→        ipnsPrivateKey: folder.ipnsKeypair.privateKey,
   389→        ipnsName: folderIpnsName,
   390→        sequenceNumber: folder.sequenceNumber,
   391→        ctx: this.ctx,
   392→      });
   393→
   394→      // 3. Update internal state
   395→      folder.children = updatedChildren;
   396→      folder.sequenceNumber = newSequenceNumber;
   397→      folder.lastLoadedAt = Date.now();

    70→ */
    71→export async function createWalletTestAccount(
    72→  browser: Browser,
    73→  name: string
    74→): Promise<WalletTestAccount> {
    75→  const account = privateKeyToAccount(generatePrivateKey());
    76→  const context = await browser.newContext();
    77→  const page = await context.newPage();
    78→
    79→  // Install mock wallet BEFORE navigating (EIP-6963 provider announcement)
    80→  await setupMockWallet(page, account);
    81→
    82→  // Drive the real wallet login flow through the UI
    83→  const result = await loginViaWallet(page, { timeout: 90_000 });
    84→  if (result.outcome !== 'success') {
    85→    throw new Error(`Wallet login for "${name}" did not reach /files (outcome: ${result.outcome})`);
    86→  }
    87→
    88→  // Wait for file list or empty state to confirm vault is accessible
    89→  await Promise.race([
    90→    page.locator('.file-list[role="grid"]').waitFor({ state: 'visible', timeout: 30_000 }),
    91→    page.locator('[data-testid="empty-state"]').waitFor({ state: 'visible', timeout: 30_000 }),
    92→  ]);
    93→
    94→  // Extract auth state from browser
    95→  const authState = await extractAuthState(page);
    96→
    97→  return {
    98→    name,
    99→    context,
   100→    page,
   101→    account,
   102→    publicKey: authState.publicKey,
   103→    accessToken: authState.accessToken,
   104→    rootIpnsName: authState.rootIpnsName,
   105→  };
   106→}
   107→
   108→/**
   109→ * Create multiple wallet-based test accounts sequentially.
   110→ * Sequential creation avoids overwhelming the Web3Auth Sapphire Devnet.
   111→ */
   112→export async function createWalletTestAccounts(
   113→  browser: Browser,
   114→  names: string[]
   115→): Promise<WalletTestAccount[]> {
   116→  const accounts: WalletTestAccount[] = [];
   117→  for (const name of names) {
   118→    accounts.push(await createWalletTestAccount(browser, name));
   119→  }
   120→  return accounts;
   121→}
   122→
   123→/**
   124→ * Close all wallet test account contexts.
   125→ */
   126→export async function closeWalletTestAccounts(accounts: WalletTestAccount[]): Promise<void> {
   127→  for (const account of accounts) {
   128→    await account.context.close();
   129→  }

   920→      userPublicKey: this.config.vaultKeypair.publicKey,

   923→
   924→  // ---- Operation wrapper ----
   925→
   926→  /**
   927→   * Wrap an async operation with event emission and error handling.
   928→   *
   929→   * Emits 'operation:start' before, 'operation:end' on success (with duration),
   930→   * and 'error' on failure. Also calls the config callbacks if provided.
   931→   */
   932→  private async withOperation<T>(name: string, fn: () => Promise<T>): Promise<T> {
   933→    const start = Date.now();
   934→    this.config.onOperationStart?.(name);
   935→    this.emitter.emit({ type: 'operation:start', operation: name });
   936→
   937→    try {
   938→      const result = await fn();
   939→      const durationMs = Date.now() - start;
   940→      this.config.onOperationEnd?.(name);
   941→      this.emitter.emit({ type: 'operation:end', operation: name, durationMs });
   942→      return result;
   943→    } catch (error) {
   944→      this.config.onError?.(error as Error);
   945→      this.emitter.emit({ type: 'error', operation: name, error: error as Error });
   946→      throw error;
   947→    }
   948→  }
   949→}

  "old_string": "  private async withOperation<T>(name: string, fn: () => Promise<T>): Promise<T> {\n    const start = Date.now();\n    this.config.onOperationStart?.(name);\n    this.emitter.emit({ type: 'operation:start', operation: name });\n\n    try {\n      const result = await fn();\n      const durationMs = Date.now() - start;\n      this.config.onOperationEnd?.(name);\n      this.emitter.emit({ type: 'operation:end', operation: name, durationMs });\n      return result;\n    } catch (error) {\n      this.config.onError?.(error as Error);\n      this.emitter.emit({ type: 'error', operation: name, error: error as Error });\n      throw error;\n    }\n  }",
  "new_string": "  private async withOperation<T>(name: string, fn: () => Promise<T>): Promise<T> {\n    const start = Date.now();\n    this.notifySafely(() => this.config.onOperationStart?.(name));\n    this.emitter.emit({ type: 'operation:start', operation: name });\n\n    try {\n      const result = await fn();\n      const durationMs = Date.now() - start;\n      this.notifySafely(() => this.config.onOperationEnd?.(name));\n      this.emitter.emit({ type: 'operation:end', operation: name, durationMs });\n      return result;\n    } catch (error) {\n      this.notifySafely(() => this.config.onError?.(error as Error));\n      this.emitter.emit({ type: 'error', operation: name, error: error as Error });\n      throw error;\n    }\n  }\n\n  private notifySafely(fn: () => void): void {\n    try {\n      fn();\n    } catch (err) {\n      console.warn('[SDK] Lifecycle callback threw:', err);\n    }\n  }"
}

  "file_path": "/Users/michael/Code/cipher-box/tests/e2e/utils/multi-account-wallet.ts",
  "old_string": "export async function createWalletTestAccount(\n  browser: Browser,\n  name: string\n): Promise<WalletTestAccount> {\n  const account = privateKeyToAccount(generatePrivateKey());\n  const context = await browser.newContext();\n  const page = await context.newPage();\n\n  // Install mock wallet BEFORE navigating (EIP-6963 provider announcement)\n  await setupMockWallet(page, account);\n\n  // Drive the real wallet login flow through the UI\n  const result = await loginViaWallet(page, { timeout: 90_000 });\n  if (result.outcome !== 'success') {\n    throw new Error(`Wallet login for \"${name}\" did not reach /files (outcome: ${result.outcome})`);\n  }\n\n  // Wait for file list or empty state to confirm vault is accessible\n  await Promise.race([\n    page.locator('.file-list[role=\"grid\"]').waitFor({ state: 'visible', timeout: 30_000 }),\n    page.locator('[data-testid=\"empty-state\"]').waitFor({ state: 'visible', timeout: 30_000 }),\n  ]);\n\n  // Extract auth state from browser\n  const authState = await extractAuthState(page);\n\n  return {\n    name,\n    context,\n    page,\n    account,\n    publicKey: authState.publicKey,\n    accessToken: authState.accessToken,\n    rootIpnsName: authState.rootIpnsName,\n  };\n}",
  "new_string": "export async function createWalletTestAccount(\n  browser: Browser,\n  name: string\n): Promise<WalletTestAccount> {\n  const account = privateKeyToAccount(generatePrivateKey());\n  const context = await browser.newContext();\n\n  try {\n    const page = await context.newPage();\n\n    // Install mock wallet BEFORE navigating (EIP-6963 provider announcement)\n    await setupMockWallet(page, account);\n\n    // Drive the real wallet login flow through the UI\n    const result = await loginViaWallet(page, { timeout: 90_000 });\n    if (result.outcome !== 'success') {\n      throw new Error(`Wallet login for \"${name}\" did not reach /files (outcome: ${result.outcome})`);\n    }\n\n    // Wait for file list or empty state to confirm vault is accessible\n    await Promise.race([\n      page.locator('.file-list[role=\"grid\"]').waitFor({ state: 'visible', timeout: 30_000 }),\n      page.locator('[data-testid=\"empty-state\"]').waitFor({ state: 'visible', timeout: 30_000 }),\n    ]);\n\n    // Extract auth state from browser\n    const authState = await extractAuthState(page);\n\n    return {\n      name,\n      context,\n      page,\n      account,\n      publicKey: authState.publicKey,\n      accessToken: authState.accessToken,\n      rootIpnsName: authState.rootIpnsName,\n    };\n  } catch (err) {\n    await context.close();\n    throw err;\n  }\n}"
}

{
  "replace_all": false,

  "old_string": "export async function createWalletTestAccounts(\n  browser: Browser,\n  names: string[]\n): Promise<WalletTestAccount[]> {\n  const accounts: WalletTestAccount[] = [];\n  for (const name of names) {\n    accounts.push(await createWalletTestAccount(browser, name));\n  }\n  return accounts;\n}",
  "new_string": "export async function createWalletTestAccounts(\n  browser: Browser,\n  names: string[]\n): Promise<WalletTestAccount[]> {\n  const accounts: WalletTestAccount[] = [];\n  try {\n    for (const name of names) {\n      accounts.push(await createWalletTestAccount(browser, name));\n    }\n    return accounts;\n  } catch (err) {\n    // Clean up already-created accounts before re-throwing\n    await closeWalletTestAccounts(accounts);\n    throw err;\n  }\n}"
}

--- Thread 1 (ID: PRRT_kwDOQ6DXvM511Xpj) ---
Author: coderabbitai
File: packages/sdk/src/client.ts:359
Comment DB ID: 2968419257
Body: _⚠️ Potential issue_ | _🟠 Major_

**Don't hand the background re-wrap the same `folderKey` buffer you return.**

`reWrapNewItems()` keeps using `folderKey` after `createFolder()` returns. Because that `Uint8Array` is also returned to the caller, any caller-side wipe or mutation before the async task finishes can produce bad wrapped keys for shared recipients. Launch the detached work with its own copy and wipe that copy when it settles.

<details>
<summary>🔧 Proposed fix</summary>

```diff
       if (this.config.shareCallbacks) {
-        // Don't await — re-wrapping shouldn't block folder creation return
-        this.reWrapNewItems(parentIpnsName, [
-          { keyType: 'folder', itemId: folder.id, plaintextKey: folderKey },
-        ]).catch((err) => {
-          console.warn('[SDK] Po

--- Thread 2 (ID: PRRT_kwDOQ6DXvM511cs4) ---
Author: copilot-pull-request-reviewer
File: apps/web/src/lib/sdk-provider.ts:85
Comment DB ID: 2968447307
Body: ensureFolderRegistered() can register a folder into the SDK while the folder is still a navigation placeholder (folderKey/ipnsPrivateKey are empty Uint8Arrays and isLoaded/isLoading reflect “not yet loaded”). This will leave the SDK with invalid key material for that ipnsName and can cause subsequent mutations/uploads to fail with confusing crypto/IPNS errors. Add a guard to only register when the folder has real key material (e.g., folder.isLoaded and key lengths are non-zero), or explicitly throw/return a user-facing error when called on an unloaded folder so callers can wait for navigation load to complete.
```

> `166-169`: _⚠️ Potential issue_ | _🟡 Minor_
> 
> **Pass `Uint8Array` directly to `Blob` instead of using `.buffer`.**
> 
> Line 168 uses `ciphertextBytes.buffer as ArrayBuffer` for Blob construction. Even though `.slice()` creates a copy with its own ArrayBuffer, the guideline explicitly prohibits using `.buffer` on `Uint8Array` for Blob construction — pass the typed array directly to avoid any risk of silent data corruption from offset views.
> 
> 
> <details>
> <summary>Proposed fix</summary>
> 
> ```diff
>        // Use .slice() to get a clean copy with its own ArrayBuffer,
>        // avoiding sub-view issues if ciphertext is an offset view.
>        const ciphertextBytes = encrypted.ciphertext.slice();
> -      const blob = new Blob([ciphertextBytes.buffer as ArrayBuffer]);
> +      const blob = new Blob([ciphertextBytes]);
>        const { cid } = await addToIpfs(blob);
> ```
> </details>
> 
> As per coding guidelines: "Never use `.buffer` on Uint8Array for Blob construction; pass the typed array directly to avoid silent data corruption from including extra bytes."
>

    50→ * Clears internal state, key caches, and event subscriptions.

    52→export function destroySdkClient(): void {
    53→  if (_client) {
    54→    _client.destroy();
    55→    _client = null;
    56→  }
    57→}
    58→
    59→/**
    60→ * Ensure a folder from the Zustand store is registered in the SDK's
    61→ * internal FolderTree. This bridges the gap between folder navigation
    62→ * (which loads folders into Zustand) and SDK operations (which require
    63→ * folders in the SDK's internal state).
    64→ *
    65→ * Only registers if the SDK doesn't already have the folder. The SDK's
    66→ * internal state (sequence number, children, keys) is authoritative once
    67→ * a folder is registered — all mutations go through the SDK.
    68→ */
    69→export function ensureFolderRegistered(folder: FolderNode): void {
    70→  const client = getSdkClient();
    71→
    72→  // Don't overwrite if SDK already has this folder — its internal state
    73→  // is authoritative (correct sequence number, keys, children from mutations)
    74→  if (client.hasFolder(folder.ipnsName)) return;
    75→
    76→  client.registerFolder(
    77→    folder.ipnsName,
    78→    folder.folderKey,
    79→    {

    81→      privateKey: folder.ipnsPrivateKey,
    82→    },
    83→    folder.children,
    84→    folder.sequenceNumber
    85→  );

Body: _⚠️ Potential issue_ | _🟡 Minor_

**Cancel the pending focus frame in this effect cleanup.**

This effect schedules a `requestAnimationFrame`, but the cleanup only flips `cancelled`. Closing the modal before the frame runs can still focus the textarea on the next paint.

<details>
<summary>🔧 Suggested fix</summary>

```diff

--- Thread 2 (ID: PRRT_kwDOQ6DXvM511o-s) ---
Author: coderabbitai
File: packages/sdk/src/client.ts:165
Comment DB ID: 2968515922
Body: _⚠️ Potential issue_ | _🟠 Major_

**Avoid sharing live folder-key buffers across the SDK boundary.**

`registerFolder()` caches caller-owned `Uint8Array`s directly, `loadFolder()` returns the same cached `FolderState` instance it stores, and `getFolderIpnsPrivateKey()` hands the live private-key buffer back out. Because `FolderTree.clear()` zeroes stored keys on `destroy()`, this can unexpectedly wipe external state; the reverse direction also lets callers mutate the SDK’s signing key in place.


<details>
<summary>🔒 Suggested direction</summary>

```diff
+  private cloneIpnsKeypair(ipnsKeypai

File: packages/sdk/src/client.ts:338
Comment DB ID: 2968515925
Body: _⚠️ Potential issue_ | _🟠 Major_

**Mirror the upload-path cleanup in `createFolder()`.**

`createSubfolder()` allocates plaintext `folderKey` and `ipnsPrivateKey`, but the duplicate-name check and both publish calls can still throw before those buffers escape to the caller. Unlike `uploadFile()`, this path never clears them on failure.


<details>
<summary>🧹 Suggested fix</summary>

```diff

Body: _⚠️ Potential issue_ | _🟠 Major_

**Harden cleanup so it never masks the original failure.**

If `context.close()` throws during error handling, it can replace the real root-cause exception and may leave later contexts unclosed.



<details>
<summary>♻️ Proposed fix</summary>

     throw err;

   286→  async createFolder(
   287→    parentIpnsName: string,
   288→    name: string
   289→  ): Promise<{ id: string; ipnsName: string; folderKey: Uint8Array; ipnsPrivateKey: Uint8Array }> {
   290→    return this.withOperation('createFolder', async () => {
   291→      const parent = this.folderTree.get(parentIpnsName);
   292→      if (!parent) throw new Error('Parent folder not loaded');
   293→
   294→      // 1. Create subfolder (generates keys, wraps with user public key)
   295→      const { folder, ipnsPrivateKey, folderKey, encryptedIpnsPrivateKey, keyEpoch } =
   296→        await sdkCore.createSubfolder({
   297→          name,
   298→          userPublicKey: this.config.vaultKeypair.publicKey,
   299→          teeKeys: this.config.teeKeys,
   300→        });
   301→
   302→      // 2. Check for duplicate name
   303→      if (parent.children.some((child) => child.name === name)) {
   304→        throw new Error('An item with this name already exists');
   305→      }
   306→
   307→      // 3. Add folder entry to parent's children
   308→      const updatedChildren: FolderChild[] = [...parent.children, folder];
   309→
   310→      // 3. Update parent metadata and publish
   311→      const { newSequenceNumber } = await sdkCore.updateFolderMetadataAndPublish({
   312→        children: updatedChildren,
   313→        folderKey: parent.folderKey,
   314→        ipnsPrivateKey: parent.ipnsKeypair.privateKey,
   315→        ipnsName: parentIpnsName,
   316→        sequenceNumber: parent.sequenceNumber,
   317→        ctx: this.ctx,
   318→        encryptedIpnsPrivateKey: encryptedIpnsPrivateKey,
   319→        keyEpoch,
   320→      });
   321→
   322→      // 4. Update parent state
   323→      parent.children = updatedChildren;
   324→      parent.sequenceNumber = newSequenceNumber;
   325→      parent.lastLoadedAt = Date.now();
   326→      this.folderTree.set(parentIpnsName, parent);
   327→
   328→      // 5. Publish empty metadata for the new subfolder
   329→      await sdkCore.updateFolderMetadataAndPublish({
   330→        children: [],
   331→        folderKey,
   332→        ipnsPrivateKey,
   333→        ipnsName: folder.ipnsName,
   334→        sequenceNumber: 0n,
   335→        ctx: this.ctx,
   336→        encryptedIpnsPrivateKey,
   337→        keyEpoch,
   338→      });
   339→
   340→      // 7. Emit parent folder updated event
   341→      this.emitter.emit({
   342→        type: 'folder:updated',
   343→        folderId: parentIpnsName,
   344→        ipnsName: parentIpnsName,
   345→        children: updatedChildren,
   346→        sequenceNumber: newSequenceNumber,
   347→      });
   348→
   349→      // 8. Re-wrap subfolder key for share recipients (non-blocking)
   350→      if (this.config.shareCallbacks) {
   351→        // Copy folderKey for the detached task — caller may wipe the returned buffer
   352→        const folderKeyCopy = new Uint8Array(folderKey);
   353→        this.reWrapNewItems(parentIpnsName, [
   354→          { keyType: 'folder', itemId: folder.id, plaintextKey: folderKeyCopy },
   355→        ])
   356→          .catch((err) => {
   357→            console.warn('[SDK] Post-createFolder re-wrapping failed:', err);
   358→          })
   359→          .finally(() => {
   360→            clearBytes(folderKeyCopy);
   361→          });
   362→      }
   363→
   364→      return { id: folder.id, ipnsName: folder.ipnsName, folderKey, ipnsPrivateKey };

    72→  browser: Browser,

    74→): Promise<WalletTestAccount> {
    75→  const account = privateKeyToAccount(generatePrivateKey());
    76→  const context = await browser.newContext();
    77→
    78→  try {
    79→    const page = await context.newPage();
    80→
    81→    // Install mock wallet BEFORE navigating (EIP-6963 provider announcement)

    89→      );
    90→    }
    91→
    92→    // Wait for file list or empty state to confirm vault is accessible

    95→      page.locator('[data-testid="empty-state"]').waitFor({ state: 'visible', timeout: 30_000 }),
    96→    ]);
    97→
    98→    // Extract auth state from browser
    99→    const authState = await extractAuthState(page);
   100→

   102→      name,
   103→      context,

   105→      account,

   107→      accessToken: authState.accessToken,
   108→      rootIpnsName: authState.rootIpnsName,
   109→    };
   110→  } catch (err) {
   111→    await context.close();
   112→    throw err;
   113→  }
   114→}
   115→
   116→/**
   117→ * Create multiple wallet-based test accounts sequentially.
   118→ * Sequential creation avoids overwhelming the Web3Auth Sapphire Devnet.
   119→ */
   120→export async function createWalletTestAccounts(
   121→  browser: Browser,
   122→  names: string[]
   123→): Promise<WalletTestAccount[]> {
   124→  const accounts: WalletTestAccount[] = [];
   125→  try {
   126→    for (const name of names) {
   127→      accounts.push(await createWalletTestAccount(browser, name));
   128→    }
   129→    return accounts;

}

Wraps @cipherbox/sdk-core\n+ * stateless functions with internal state management (FolderTree, KeyCache).\n+ *\n+ * Design principles:\n+ * - Zero React/Zustand/browser dependencies\n+ * - All state flows through typed events (SdkEvent)\n+ * - Operations are wrapped with withOperation() for consistent\n+ *   start/end/error event emission\n+ * - Key material is cleared on destroy()\n+ */\n+\n+import type { SdkContext, ProgressCallback, DownloadProgressCallback } from '@cipherbox/sdk-core';\n+import * as sdkCore from '@cipherbox/sdk-core';\n+import { clearBytes } from '@cipherbox/crypto';\n+import type { FolderChild } from '@cipherbox/core';\n+import type { CipherBoxClientConfig, FolderState } from './types';\n+import { SdkEventEmitter, type SdkEvent, type SdkEventHandler } from './events';\n+import { FolderTree } from './state/folder-tree';\n+import { KeyCache } from './state/key-cache';\n+import * as binOps from './bin';\n+import type { BinState } from './bin';\n+import * as shareOps from './share';\n+import type { SentShareInfo } from './share';\n+\n+/** Thrown when a bin operation is attempted before loadBin() has been called. */\n+export class BinNotLoadedError extends Error {\n+  constructor() {\n+    super('Bin not loaded');\n+    this.name = 'BinNotLoadedError';\n+  }\n+}\n+\n+export class CipherBoxClient {\n+  private config: CipherBoxClientConfig;\n+  private ctx: SdkContext;\n+  private emitter: SdkEventEmitter;\n+  private folderTree: FolderTree;\n+  private keyCache: KeyCache;\n+  private binState: BinState | null = null;\n+  /** Internal copies of key material — zeroed on destroy() without affecting caller buffers */\n+  private internalVaultKeypair: { publicKey: Uint8Array; privateKey: Uint8Array };\n+  private internalRootFolderKey: Uint8Array;\n+\n+  constructor(config: CipherBoxClientConfig) {\n+    // Defensive copy of key material so destroy() only zeroes our copies\n+    this.internalVaultKeypair = {\n+      publicKey: new Uint8Array(config.vaultKeypair.publicKey),\n+      privateKey: new Uint8Array(config.vaultKeypair.privateKey),\n+    };\n+    this.internalRootFolderKey = new Uint8Array(config.rootFolderKey);\n+    this.config = {\n+      ...config,\n+      vaultKeypair: this.internalVaultKeypair,\n+      rootFolderKey: this.internalRootFolderKey,\n+    };\n+    this.ctx = {\n+      apiUrl: config.apiUrl,\n+      getAccessToken: config.getAccessToken,\n+    };\n+    this.emitter = new SdkEventEmitter();\n+    this.folderTree = new FolderTree();\n+    this.keyCache = new KeyCache();\n+  }\n+\n+  // ---- Event subscription ----\n+\n+  /** Subscribe to SDK events. Returns an unsubscribe function. */\n+  on(handler: SdkEventHandler): () => void {\n+    return this.emitter.on(handler);\n+  }\n+\n+  /** Unsubscribe a previously registered handler. */\n+  off(handler: SdkEventHandler): void {\n+    this.emitter.off(handler);\n+  }\n+\n+  // ---- Lifecycle ----\n+\n+  // ---- Share re-wrapping (internal) ----\n+\n+  /**\n+   * Re-wrap keys for share recipients after adding items to a folder.\n+   *\n+   * Queries the share callbacks for active shares covering the folder,\n+   * then wraps each new item's plaintext key for each recipient.\n+   * Failures are logged but do not propagate (re-wrapping is best-effort).\n+   */\n+  private async reWrapNewItems(\n+    folderIpnsName: string,\n+    items: Array<{ keyType: 'file' | 'folder'; itemId: string; plaintextKey: Uint8Array }>\n+  ): Promise<void> {\n+    const callbacks = this.config.shareCallbacks;\n+    if (!callbacks) return;\n+\n+    const coveringShares = await callbacks.getCoveringShares(folderIpnsName);\n+    if (coveringShares.length === 0) return;\n+\n+    const { failedRecipients } = await shareOps.reWrapForRecipients({\n+      coveringShares,\n+      newItems: items,\n+      addShareKeysFn: callbacks.addShareKeys,\n+    });\n+\n+    if (failedRecipients.length > 0) {\n+      console.warn(\n+        `[SDK] Re-wrapping failed for ${failedRecipients.length} recipient(s):`,\n+        failedRecipients.map((k) => k.slice(0, 10) + '...')\n+      );\n+      this.emitter.emit({\n+        type: 'share:reWrapFailed',\n+        folderIpnsName,\n+        failedRecipients,\n+      } as SdkEvent);\n+    }\n+  }\n+\n+  /**\n+   * Destroy the client, clearing all sensitive state.\n+   * After calling destroy(), the client instance should not be reused.\n+   */\n+  destroy(): void {\n+    this.folderTree.clear();\n+    this.keyCache.clear();\n+    this.emitter.removeAll();\n+    // Zero internal key copies publicKey,\n+      accessToken: authState.accessToken,\n+      rootIpnsName: vaultState.rootIpnsName,\n+    };\n+  });\n+}\n+\n+/**\n+ * Create a test account with a random wallet identity and > AGENT

It's used in `vault-ipns.test.ts` for test-only imports (`deriveRegistryIpnsKeypair`, `initializeVault`). The test verifies that the crypto package's `deriveVaultIpnsKeypair` is consistent with core's vault initialization. So `devDependencies` is correct — it's only needed for tests, not at runtime.

The architecture diagram in the README also shows `@cipherbox/core` depends on `@cipherbox/crypto` (not the reverse), so no circular runtime dependency exists. The devDep is just for cross-package test verification.

The build command uses `pnpm --filter ... build` which builds all filters in parallel, but these packages have dependency ordering. Need to build them sequentially or use `pnpm`'s topological build. Let me also check the `deploy-staging.yml` since it only builds crypto too:

> AGENT

The E2E workflow only builds `@cipherbox/crypto`, `@cipherbox/api`, and `@cipherbox/web` — but the web app now depends on four new shared packages (`core`, `api-client`, `sdk-core`, `sdk`) that were never being built. Same issue in `deploy-staging.yml` (web + 3 desktop platform builds) and `e2e-desktop.yml`.

Fixed all three workflows to build the full package dependency chain in order before the web/desktop builds.

> AGENT

This is a first-release issue. The config is set up correctly for independent versioning — each package is a separate `component` with its own entry in the manifest and `include-component-in-tag: true` (so they get tags like `@cipherbox/core-v0.25.0`).

The reason they're all bumping together in PR #298 is that the single PR #296 touched all packages simultaneously (it created them all). Release Please attributes that commit to every package it changed, so they all get the same `feat` bump.

Going forward, a commit that only touches `packages/sdk/` will only bump `@cipherbox/sdk`, a commit only in `packages/core/` will only bump `@cipherbox/core`, etc. They'll diverge naturally as changes are made independently.

The root package (`.`) also bumped because `apps/web` and `apps/api` changes are attributed to it, and its `extra-files` list propagates the root version to `apps/*/package.json` and `packages/crypto/package.json` — but the four new packages are **not** in that `extra-files` list, so they'll version independently.

> DEVELOPER

Is it possible to change the name of the PR that release please creates?

> AGENT

Yes — it's already configured in your `release-please-config.json`. The root package has:

```json
"pull-request-title-pattern": "chore: release ${version}"
```

That's why PR #298 is titled "chore: release main". You can customize it with these placeholders:

- `${version}` — the version number
- `${component}` — the component name

For example, to get "chore: release v0.25.0":

```json
"pull-request-title-pattern": "chore: release v${version}"
```

> AGENT

The workflow config looks fine. The issue is that `pull-request-title-pattern` only controls the **root component's** title pattern. When release-please has a **multi-component** manifest (like yours with 5 packages), it creates a single PR for all components and uses the `target-branch` name ("main") as the title suffix instead of a single version — because there's no single version to show.

The fix is to set `pull-request-title-pattern` at the **top level** of the config (outside `packages`), or adjust the pattern to not use `${version}` since it's ambiguous with multiple components. For example:

```json
"pull-request-title-pattern": "chore: release"
```

Or if you want it to say something more specific:

```json
"pull-request-title-pattern": "chore: release ${branch}"
```

Want me to update it, or do you have a preferred title format?

> AGENT

This isn't the build failure anymore — the build is passing now. These are **E2E test failures** at runtime. The key error is:

```
Error: Upload failed with alert: Folder not loaded
```

This is the `ensureFolderRegistered` guard we added — it's now silently skipping registration of folders that aren't fully loaded, so when the upload tries to use the SDK, the folder isn't registered.

    59→/**
    60→ * Ensure a folder from the Zustand store is registered in the SDK's
    61→ * internal FolderTree. This bridges the gap between folder navigation
    62→ * (which loads folders into Zustand) and SDK operations (which require
    63→ * folders in the SDK's internal state).
    64→ *
    65→ * Only registers if the SDK doesn't already have the folder. The SDK's
    66→ * internal state (sequence number, children, keys) is authoritative once
    67→ * a folder is registered — all mutations go through the SDK.
    68→ */
    69→export function ensureFolderRegistered(folder: FolderNode): void {
    70→  const client = getSdkClient();
    71→
    72→  // Don't overwrite if SDK already has this folder — its internal state
    73→  // is authoritative (correct sequence number, keys, children from mutations)
    74→  if (client.hasFolder(folder.ipnsName)) return;
    75→
    76→  // Guard: don't register placeholder folders that haven't loaded yet —
    77→  // empty key material would cause crypto/IPNS errors on subsequent mutations
    78→  if (!folder.isLoaded || folder.folderKey.length === 0 || folder.ipnsPrivateKey.length === 0) {
    79→    return;
    80→  }
    81→
    82→  client.registerFolder(
    83→    folder.ipnsName,
    84→    folder.folderKey,
    85→    {
    86→      publicKey: new Uint8Array(0), // Public key derived from private key when needed
    87→      privateKey: folder.ipnsPrivateKey,
    88→    },
    89→    folder.children,
    90→    folder.sequenceNumber
    91→  );
    92→}
    93→

> AGENT

The problem is that by the time `ensureFolderRegistered` is called in the mutation hooks, the folder **is** loaded (it has keys and children). The `isLoaded` check is too strict — the folder store may have a folder that was loaded via navigation (has all keys) but `isLoaded` could be false in an edge case, or the folder may have been loaded by the SDK itself (via `createFolder`) and never set `isLoaded` in the Zustand store.

Let me check what `isLoaded` actually means in the calling context:

> AGENT

The issue is clear. When `ensureFolderRegistered` silently returns without registering (because of the guard), the subsequent `client.createFolder()` throws "Parent folder not loaded" → which surfaces as "Folder not loaded" in the UI. The guard should only check for empty key material, not `isLoaded` — by the time mutations run, the folder has valid keys even if `isLoaded` hasn't been set in every code path.

> AGENT

PR #301: https://github.com/FSM1/cipher-box/pull/301

The root cause: the `isLoaded` flag we added to the guard (from the Copilot review comment) isn't set in all code paths — SDK-created folders and the root folder after vault init have valid keys but `isLoaded` may be false. The guard silently returned without registering, so every subsequent mutation hit "Folder not loaded". Removed the `isLoaded` check, keeping only the empty key material check which is the actual safety condition.

> AGENT

It's the session restore after `page.reload()` — hitting the 360s timeout at the `waitForFunction` checking for vault readiness (line 557). The browser/context was closed (likely by Playwright's test timeout), so the Zustand stores never came back.

94 passed, only test 3.7 failed, and it timed out at the full 360s. This is a CI-specific flakiness issue — the Core Kit session restore on Sapphire Devnet is sometimes extremely slow or fails entirely on CI runners.

This isn't related to the `ensureFolderRegistered` fix. It's the pre-existing reload flakiness on CI. The "Folder not loaded" errors from before are gone (94 tests passed vs 6 failing previously).

Want me to look into making 3.7 more resilient, or is this a known flaky test you're OK retrying?

> DEVELOPER

going to retry it and hopefully its just flakiness that will resolve itself, but i will let you know if we need to try and fix it asap

> AGENT

Sounds good. The fact that it went from 6 failures to just the 3.7 reload timeout is a good sign — that one has always been sensitive to Sapphire Devnet latency on CI. Let me know how the retry goes.

> AGENT

There's the root cause. The old flow used `reinjectTestAuthAfterReload(page)` — which manually re-injected the test auth state after reload, making it instant. The new wallet-based flow removed that and relies on Core Kit to auto-restore the session from localStorage after reload. Let me check what `reinjectTestAuthAfterReload` did:

> TOOL

tool_use Bash
id: toolu_01L9bjRDE7mwPKP9sgpiQT41
```json
{
  "command": "git show 4696a2b665:tests/e2e/utils/web3auth-helpers.ts 2>&1 | grep -A30 \"reinjectTestAuthAfterReload\"",
  "description": "Check old reinject helper"
}
```

> TOOL

tool_result
id: toolu_01L9bjRDE7mwPKP9sgpiQT41
```
export async function reinjectTestAuthAfterReload(
  page: Page,
  state?: TestAuthState
): Promise<void> {
  const authState = state ?? cachedTestAuthState;

  // If not using test-login, Core Kit handles session restore
  if (!process.env.TEST_LOGIN_SECRET || !authState) return;

  // Try to refresh the access token via the cookie
  const apiBase = process.env.API_BASE_URL || 'http://localhost:3000';
  const refreshResponse = await page.request.post(`${apiBase}/auth/refresh`);

  if (refreshResponse.ok()) {
    const { accessToken, email } = await refreshResponse.json();
    authState.accessToken = accessToken;
    if (email) authState.email = email;
  }

  await injectAuthState(page, authState);

  // Navigate to files via hash router
  await page.evaluate(() => {
    window.location.hash = '#/files';
  });
  await page.waitForURL('**/files', { timeout: 30000 });
}

/**
 * Gets the current storage state (cookies and localStorage).
 * Useful for saving authenticated session state.
```

> AGENT

Here's the problem. After `page.reload()`, the mock wallet is gone — it was injected via `page.addInitScript` or similar, which runs on initial page load. But after reload, Core Kit needs to:

1. Restore its session from localStorage (the DKG shares are stored there)
2. Call `exportTssKey()` against Sapphire Devnet
3. But it also needs the wallet connected to verify the session

The mock wallet may not survive the reload. Let me check:

`addInitScript` persists across reloads — Playwright re-runs init scripts on every navigation/reload. And `exposeFunction` also persists on the browser context. So the mock wallet **does** survive reload.

> AGENT

The error said it died at `page.waitForFunction` (line 557) with "Target page, context or browser has been closed" after the full 360s timeout. That means the user-menu check at line 549 passed (took up to 120s), but the vault readiness check never completed within 60s, and then the overall test timeout of 360s killed the browser.

Now I see the full picture. After reload:

1. `initCoreKit()` runs → calls `ck.init()` → if Core Kit finds a stored session, status = `LOGGED_IN`
2. The `restoreSession` effect fires because `coreKitLoggedIn && !isAuthenticated`
3. It calls `authApi.refresh()` to get a new access token from the HTTP-only cookie
4. Then `initializeOrLoadVaultRef.current?.()` to load vault keys + init SDK
5. Only then does `setAuthenticated()` fire, which makes the user-menu visible

The question is: where does this fail? The error message said it timed out at the `waitForFunction` checking vault stores — meaning the user-menu appeared (line 549 passed) but vault never got `rootFolderKey`. Let me check if there's a redirect issue — maybe the app redirected to `/` after reload instead of staying on `/files`:

Wait — let me re-read the error more carefully. The failure message was:

> `page.waitForFunction: Target page, context or browser has been closed`

This means the 360s **test timeout** killed the browser context while the `waitForFunction` was still running. The user-menu check (120s timeout) passed, but the vault `waitForFunction` (60s timeout) didn't complete within its 60s — and then the remaining test time ran out at 360s total.

But 120s + 60s = 180s, far less than 360s. Unless the user-menu wait took close to the full 120s, and then the vault check took the full 60s, and then something else... Actually, the `waitForFunction` itself has a 60s timeout, but the error says the test's 360s timeout expired. That means the user-menu wait took a very long time (close to 300s?), leaving almost no time for the vault check.

> AGENT

This is behind `import.meta.env.DEV` — but CI runs a production build (`pnpm --filter @cipherbox/web build`), not dev mode. So `__ZUSTAND_STORES` **is never set** in the CI build.

That's the root cause. The `waitForFunction` checking `window.__ZUSTAND_STORES?.vault` will **never** succeed in CI because the stores are only exposed in dev mode. The old test flow didn't need this check — it used `reinjectTestAuthAfterReload` instead.

The fix: either expose `__ZUSTAND_STORES` in all environments (or behind a test flag), or change the E2E test to not rely on Zustand store internals and instead wait for a visible UI indicator that vault is ready.

Confirmed — `pnpm --filter @cipherbox/web build` is a production build. `import.meta.env.DEV` is false.

But wait — the old test on commit `4696a2b665` also had this same `import.meta.env.DEV` guard, and **it passed**. The difference is the old test didn't have the `waitForFunction` checking `__ZUSTAND_STORES` — it used `reinjectTestAuthAfterReload` instead.

The simplest fix: the `waitForFunction` in test 3.7 should wait for a UI element that indicates vault readiness instead of checking Zustand internals. The file list appearing (which test 3.7 already waits for at line 575) is itself proof that vault loaded. So we can remove the `waitForFunction` check entirely — or replace it with waiting for the file list.

Actually, even simpler — just remove the `waitForFunction` block. The next line already waits for `workspaceFolder` to appear in the file list with a 120s timeout, which can only happen after vault + IPNS sync completes.

> AGENT

Multiple tests rely on `__ZUSTAND_STORES`. This will break other tests too (conflict-detection, multi-account-wallet, etc.). The right fix is to expose the stores in all environments, not just dev mode. It's E2E test infrastructure — there's no security risk since it's just read access to store state.

> AGENT

PR #302: https://github.com/FSM1/cipher-box/pull/302

**Root cause**: `__ZUSTAND_STORES` was

> DEVELOPER

Is there any way to reduce reliance on zustand store access in the e2e tests? it really shouldn't be necessary. using visual state should be enough.

> AGENT

You're right. The Zustand store access in E2E tests is used for two things:

1. **Waiting for vault readiness** (test 3.7) — but the file list appearing already proves this
2. **Extracting auth state** (public keys, access tokens, root IPNS name) — used by multi-account wallet tests for direct API calls and sharing workflows

The first case is easy to eliminate — just wait for UI elements. The second is harder because tests like sharing-workflow need the raw public key to call the sharing API directly.

Let me check exactly where store access is used and what each one actually needs:

> TOOL

Now I have all the context. Let me analyze each `__ZUSTAND_STORES` usage systematically:

## Summary Report: `__ZUSTAND_STORES` Usage Analysis

### 1. **conflict-detection.spec.ts, Line 82**
**Context:** In `beforeAll` hook after wallet login
```
Stores accessed:
  - auth.getState().accessToken
  - vault.getState().rootIpnsName

Purpose:
  - Extract access token and root IPNS name to pass to bumpServerSequence() 
    API helper function which simulates another device publishing (bumps server sequence)

Usage:
  - Both values are used to make direct HTTP API calls to trigger conflicts
  - accessToken authorizes the conflict helper request
  - rootIpnsName identifies which IPNS record to bump on server

Could be replaced by UI approach:
  - NO. The whole point is to simulate *server-side* state change without 
    the client knowing about it. This requires direct API access outside 
    the normal UI flow. There's no visual way to observe "server sequence 
    was bumped" — you can only know by trying to publish and getting a 409.
```

### 2. **conflict-detection.spec.ts, Lines 151 & 166**
**Context:** Two helper functions `getAccessToken()` and `getRootIpnsName()`
```
Stores accessed:
  - auth.getState().accessToken
  - vault.getState().rootIpnsName

Purpose:
  - Refresh the token before each test (tokens may be refreshed between tests)
  - Get fresh IPNS name in case it changed
  - Used in the same bumpServerSequence() calls as line 82

Usage:
  - Called once per test that does conflict injection
  - Allows per-test token refresh (testable race conditions)

Could be replaced by UI approach:
  - NO. Same reasoning as line 82 — requires direct API calls that 
    are intentionally outside the normal UI flow.
```

### 3. **full-workflow.spec.ts, Line 564**
**Context:** In test `3.7 Page reload preserves session`, waiting for SDK initialization
```
Stores accessed:
  - vault.getState().rootFolderKey
  - vault.getState().rootIpnsKeypair
  - vault.getState().rootIpnsName

Purpose:
  - Poll for vault initialization to complete after page reload
  - These three fields indicate that initializeOrLoadVault() finished and 
    SDK client is ready for file operations

Usage:
  - page.waitForFunction() that reads Zustand directly (no UI indicator)
  - Required because SDK init can take 10-60s on staging, and there's 
    no visual indicator when it completes

Could be replaced by UI approach:
  - MAYBE. The test could instead wait for "first file to appear" or 
    "file list to become interactive" rather than polling internal state. 
    BUT the current approach is more precise — it waits specifically for 
    crypto keys to be loaded, not just for the list to render (which might 
    be stale/empty). A UI wait would be more fragile if the vault is empty 
    (no items to wait for) or if IPNS resolution is very slow (file list 
    renders empty then gets populated).
    VERDICT: COULD migrate to UI, but current approach is more robust.
```

### 4. **recycle-bin.spec.ts, Lines 48 & 369**
**Context:** Line 48 is a helper type definition; Line 369 is in test `TC07` reading quota
```
Stores accessed:
  - quota.getState().usedBytes

Purpose:
  - Read storage quota used bytes from Zustand quota store
  - Check that permanent delete actually reclaimed storage
  - This is a "best-effort" check with fallback (returns null if store not exposed)

Usage:
  - Line 48: Defines a helper function getQuotaUsedBytes() that safely tries 
    to read quota, catches errors, returns null
  - Line 369: Called to snapshot quota before/after permanent delete operations
  - The test passes even if quota store isn't accessible (not a hard assertion)

Could be replaced by UI approach:
  - MAYBE. The storage quota IS displayed in the UI ("[data-testid="storage-quota"]") 
    and was already tested in full-workflow.spec.ts tests 3.6.1 and 3.7.1 
    using getComputedStyle. The Zustand approach is just more direct/precise. 
    The test explicitly documents this as "best-effort" with fallback.
    VERDICT: COULD migrate to reading computed style of quota display, but 
    would be more brittle if quota element doesn't update immediately after unpin.
```

### 5. **multi-account-wallet.ts, Line 34**
**Context:** In helper function `extractAuthState()` called after wallet login
```
Stores accessed:
  - auth.getState().accessToken
  - auth.getState().vaultKeypair?.publicKey
  - vault.getState().rootIpnsName

Purpose:
  - Extract auth state to populate WalletTestAccount interface
  - publicKey is used for sharing (file sharing between accounts)
  - accessToken is used for direct API calls
  - rootIpnsName is used for conflict detection API calls

Usage:
  - Called once per test account after wallet login
  - Returned values stored in account object for entire test
  - publicKey, accessToken, rootIpnsName later used to make direct API calls 
    in multi-account sharing tests

Could be replaced by UI approach:
  - NO. These fields are specifically needed for programmatic API calls 
    (sharing, conflict detection) that happen outside the normal UI flow. 
    There's no way to "visually extract" a public key or access token.
```

### 6. **wallet-login-helpers.ts, Line 166**
**Context:** In `loginViaWallet()` function, waiting for vault init after successful login
```
Stores accessed:
  - vault.getState().rootFolderKey
  - vault.getState().rootIpnsKeypair
  - vault.getState().rootIpnsName

Purpose:
  - Same as full-workflow.spec.ts line 564
  - Poll for vault init to complete before returning from login function
  - Prevents race condition where test tries file operations before SDK ready

Usage:
  - page.waitForFunction() that reads Zustand
  - Called in every test that does wallet login
  - Without this, tests would race with async vault initialization

Could be replaced by UI approach:
  - MAYBE. Could wait for file list to render instead. But this is the 
    "canonical" way to detect SDK readiness (crypto keys loaded), not just 
    UI rendering. Same tradeoff as #3.
    VERDICT: COULD migrate, but less precise.
```

### 7. **web3auth-helpers.ts, Lines 233 & 242**
**Context:** In `injectAuthState()` function, waiting for stores to be exposed
```
Line 233:
  - Accesses: window.__ZUSTAND_STORES (checking if it exists)
  - Purpose: Verify stores are exposed before injecting state
  - Not reading store content, just checking it exists

Line 242:
  - Accesses: auth store's setState() and vault store's getState().setVaultKeys()
  - Purpose: Inject TestAuthState into stores after test-login
  - Writes to stores (not just reading)

Usage:
  - Line 232-237: Waits for stores to exist (timeout 15s) before proceeding
  - Line 241-267: Injects auth + vault state into stores to simulate login 
    without Core Kit (test-login endpoint bypass)

Could be replaced by UI approach:
  - NO. This is injecting internal state for test infrastructure, not reading it. 
    There's no UI action that would inject raw auth state into Zustand. 
    This is pure test scaffolding.
```

**Key Finding:** 5/7 usages **cannot** be replaced by UI approaches because they:
1. Make **direct API calls outside the normal UI flow** (conflict injection, sharing)
2. **Inject test infrastructure state** (state injection for test-login bypass)

Only **2/7 usages** (SDK init polling) could potentially migrate to UI waits, but would lose precision and become more fragile.

> AGENT

Here's the breakdown:

**Can't eliminate store access (5 of 7 usages):**
- **conflict-detection** & **multi-account-wallet** — need `accessToken` and `rootIpnsName` to make direct API calls outside the UI (conflict injection, sharing). No visual equivalent.
- **web3auth-helpers** — *writes* to stores to inject test state. Pure infrastructure.

**Could use UI instead (2 of 7):**
- **full-workflow test 3.7** — polls vault for `rootFolderKey`. Could just wait for the file list to appear instead (which already happens on the next line).
- **wallet-login-helpers** — same vault readiness poll after login. Could wait for the file list/empty state instead (which the caller already does).

So realistically, store exposure is necessary — the sharing and conflict tests need raw tokens and IPNS names for direct API calls. But we can eliminate the two "vault readiness" polls and replace them with UI waits.

The immediate fix is still to expose `__ZUSTAND_STORES` in production builds since those API-driven tests genuinely need it. Want me to:

1. Keep the PR as-is (expose stores in all builds), or
2. Do a hybrid — expose stores in all builds for the API tests, but also replace the two vault readiness polls with UI waits so they don't depend on it?

> AGENT

So here's the full picture:

**Sharing tests** — only need `publicKey`, which **is visible in the UI** on the Settings page. These could absolutely navigate to Settings, read the key from `.settings-pubkey-value`, and navigate back. No Zustand access needed.

**Conflict detection tests** — need `accessToken` and `rootIpnsName` to call a server API that simulates another device. These are inherently non-UI operations. But even these could potentially use `page.evaluate(() => fetch(...))` from the browser context (cookies auto-included) instead of needing the raw token. The `rootIpnsName` is the harder one — it's not displayed in the UI anywhere.

**Vault readiness polls** — completely replaceable with UI waits (file list appearing).

So the answer is: yes, the sharing tests absolutely could use the Settings page UI to get the public key. The only tests that truly need store access are:
- Conflict detection (needs `rootIpnsName` which isn't in the UI)
- The `web3auth-helpers` state injection (test infrastructure)

Want me to refactor the sharing/multi-account flow to extract the public key from the Settings page UI instead?

> DEVELOPER

ok, and the reason we havent seen the conflict detection tests failing is because they are part of the sequential 30+ tests that did not run after 3.7 failed?

> AGENT

Exactly. The full-workflow tests run as `test.describe.serial` — when 3.7 fails, all 36 subsequent tests are skipped. And the conflict-detection, sharing-workflow, and other spec files that use `__ZUSTAND_STORES` also fail in the same run but we've been focused on 3.7 as the first failure.

Looking back at the failing run output:

```
6 failed
94 passed
36 did not run
```

Those 6 failures likely include conflict-detection and sharing-workflow hitting the same `__ZUSTAND_STORES` issue. The fix needs to cover all of them, not just 3.7.

So the plan should be:
1. **Expose `__ZUSTAND_STORES` in all builds** — needed for conflict-detection (`rootIpnsName`) and web3auth-helpers (state injection)
2. **Replace vault readiness polls** (full-workflow 3.7, wallet-login-helpers) with UI waits
3. **Extract public keys from Settings UI** in sharing/multi-account tests instead of from Zustand

Want me to tackle all three, or start with just #1 (the minimal fix) to unblock CI?

> DEVELOPER

I would really prefer not exposing zustand stores in production builds. why can the tests not inject a CI env flag?

> AGENT

Good call. The web app build already receives env vars like `VITE_WEB3AUTH_CLIENT_ID` and `VITE_API_URL` from the CI workflow. We just need to add something like `VITE_E2E=true` and gate the store exposure on that:

```typescript
if (import.meta.env.DEV || import.meta.env.VITE_E2E) {
```

The CI workflow already passes env vars to the web build step:

```yaml
- name: Build packages (excluding desktop)
  run: |
    ...
    pnpm --filter @cipherbox/web build
  env:
    VITE_WEB3AUTH_CLIENT_ID: ${{ vars.VITE_WEB3AUTH_CLIENT_ID }}
    VITE_API_URL: http://localhost:3000
    # just add:
    VITE_E2E: true
```

Vite bakes `VITE_*` env vars into the build at compile time, so it gets tree-shaken out of production deploys (where `VITE_E2E` isn't set). Want me to implement this?

     1→import { Browser, BrowserContext, Page } from '@playwright/test';
     2→import { generatePrivateKey, privateKeyToAccount, type PrivateKeyAccount } from 'viem/accounts';
     3→import { setupMockWallet, loginViaWallet } from './wallet-login-helpers';
     4→
     5→/**
     6→ * A fully authenticated test account using wallet-based login.
     7→ * Each account has an isolated browser context, a random EVM identity,
     8→ * and its public key extracted from the Zustand auth store after login.
     9→ */
    10→export interface WalletTestAccount {
    11→  name: string;
    12→  context: BrowserContext;
    13→  page: Page;
    14→  account: PrivateKeyAccount;
    15→  /** 0x04-prefixed secp256k1 public key (for sharing — extracted from auth store) */
    16→  publicKey: string;
    17→  /** JWT access token (extracted from auth store — for direct API calls) */
    18→  accessToken: string;
    19→  /** Root IPNS name (extracted from vault store — for conflict detection API calls) */
    20→  rootIpnsName: string;
    21→}
    22→
    23→/**
    24→ * Extract auth state from Zustand stores in the browser.
    25→ *
    26→ * After wallet login, Core Kit manages the secp256k1 identity in-browser.
    27→ * This function extracts the public key, access token, and vault metadata
    28→ * needed by tests that make direct API calls (conflict detection, sharing).
    29→ */
    30→async function extractAuthState(
    31→  page: Page
    32→): Promise<{ publicKey: string; accessToken: string; rootIpnsName: string }> {
    33→  return page.evaluate(() => {
    34→    const stores = (window as unknown as Record<string, unknown>).__ZUSTAND_STORES as {
    35→      auth: { getState: () => { accessToken: string; vaultKeypair?: { publicKey: Uint8Array } } };
    36→      vault: { getState: () => { rootIpnsName: string } };
    37→    };
    38→
    39→    const authState = stores.auth.getState();
    40→    const vaultState = stores.vault.getState();
    41→
    42→    // Convert Uint8Array public key to 0x-prefixed hex
    43→    const pubKeyBytes = authState.vaultKeypair?.publicKey;
    44→    let publicKey = '';
    45→    if (pubKeyBytes) {
    46→      const hex = Array.from(pubKeyBytes)
    47→        .map((b: number) => b.toString(16).padStart(2, '0'))
    48→        .join('');
    49→      publicKey = '0x' + hex;
    50→    }
    51→
    52→    return {
    53→      publicKey,
    54→      accessToken: authState.accessToken,
    55→      rootIpnsName: vaultState.rootIpnsName,
    56→    };
    57→  });
    58→}
    59→
    60→/**
    61→ * Create a test account with a random wallet identity and authenticated browser context.
    62→ *
    63→ * Uses @johanneskares/wallet-mock to inject an EIP-6963 mock provider,
    64→ * then drives the real wallet -> SIWE -> Core Kit login flow through the UI.
    65→ * Each account gets a unique random private key -> unique wallet address ->
    66→ * unique backend userId -> fresh DKG identity on Sapphire Devnet.
    67→ *
    68→ * @param browser - Playwright browser instance
    69→ * @param name - Human-readable name for this account (e.g., "alice")
    70→ */
    71→export async function createWalletTestAccount(
    72→  browser: Browser,
    73→  name: string
    74→): Promise<WalletTestAccount> {
    75→  const account = privateKeyToAccount(generatePrivateKey());
    76→  const context = await browser.newContext();
    77→
    78→  try {
    79→    const page = await context.newPage();
    80→
    81→    // Install mock wallet BEFORE navigating (EIP-6963 provider announcement)
    82→    await setupMockWallet(page, account);
    83→
    84→    // Drive the real wallet login flow through the UI
    85→    const result = await loginViaWallet(page, { timeout: 90_000 });
    86→    if (result.outcome !== 'success') {
    87→      throw new Error(
    88→        `Wallet login for "${name}" did not reach /files (outcome: ${result.outcome})`
    89→      );
    90→    }
    91→
    92→    // Wait for file list or empty state to confirm vault is accessible
    93→    await Promise.race([
    94→      page.locator('.file-list[role="grid"]').waitFor({ state: 'visible', timeout: 30_000 }),
    95→      page.locator('[data-testid="empty-state"]').waitFor({ state: 'visible', timeout: 30_000 }),
    96→    ]);
    97→
    98→    // Extract auth state from browser
    99→    const authState = await extractAuthState(page);
   100→
   101→    return {
   102→      name,
   103→      context,
   104→      page,
   105→      account,
   106→      publicKey: authState.publicKey,
   107→      accessToken: authState.accessToken,
   108→      rootIpnsName: authState.rootIpnsName,
   109→    };
   110→  } catch (err) {
   111→    await context.close().catch(() => undefined);
   112→    throw err;
   113→  }
   114→}
   115→
   116→/**
   117→ * Create multiple wallet-based test accounts sequentially.
   118→ * Sequential creation avoids overwhelming the Web3Auth Sapphire Devnet.
   119→ */
   120→export async function createWalletTestAccounts(
   121→  browser: Browser,
   122→  names: string[]
   123→): Promise<WalletTestAccount[]> {
   124→  const accounts: WalletTestAccount[] = [];
   125→  try {
   126→    for (const name of names) {
   127→      accounts.push(await createWalletTestAccount(browser, name));
   128→    }
   129→    return accounts;
   130→  } catch (err) {
   131→    // Clean up already-created accounts before re-throwing
   132→    await closeWalletTestAccounts(accounts).catch(() => undefined);
   133→    throw err;
   134→  }
   135→}
   136→
   137→/**
   138→ * Close all wallet test account contexts.
   139→ */
   140→export async function closeWalletTestAccounts(accounts: WalletTestAccount[]): Promise<void> {
   141→  for (const account of accounts) {
   142→    await account.context.close();
   143→  }
   144→}
   145→
   146→/**
   147→ * Navigate a wallet test account's page to the shared files view.
   148→ * Navigates away first to force a component remount and fresh data fetch.
   149→ */
   150→export async function navigateToShared(account: WalletTestAccount): Promise<void> {
   151→  const currentUrl = account.page.url();
   152→  if (currentUrl.includes('/shared')) {
   153→    await account.page.evaluate(() => {
   154→      window.location.hash = '#/files';
   155→    });
   156→    await account.page.waitForURL('**/files', { timeout: 30000 });
   157→  }
   158→  await account.page.evaluate(() => {
   159→    window.location.hash = '#/shared';
   160→  });
   161→  await account.page.waitForURL('**/shared', { timeout: 30000 });
   162→}
   163→
   164→/**
   165→ * Navigate a wallet test account's page to their own files.
   166→ * Navigates away first to force a component remount and fresh data fetch.
   167→ */
   168→export async function navigateToFiles(account: WalletTestAccount): Promise<void> {
   169→  const currentUrl = account.page.url();
   170→  if (currentUrl.includes('/files')) {
   171→    await account.page.evaluate(() => {
   172→      window.location.hash = '#/shared';
   173→    });
   174→    await account.page.waitForURL('**/shared', { timeout: 30000 });
   175→  }
   176→  await account.page.evaluate(() => {
   177→    window.location.hash = '#/files';
   178→  });
   179→  await account.page.waitForURL('**/files', { timeout: 30000 });
   180→}
   181→

{

  "file_path": "/Users/michael/Code/cipher-box/tests/e2e/utils/multi-account-wallet.ts",
  "old_string": "/**\n * A fully authenticated test account using wallet-based login.\n * Each account has an isolated browser context, a random EVM identity,\n * and its public key extracted from the Zustand auth store after login.\n */\nexport interface WalletTestAccount {\n  name: string;\n  context: BrowserContext;\n  page: Page;\n  account: PrivateKeyAccount;\n  /** 0x04-prefixed secp256k1 public key (for sharing — extracted from auth store) */\n  publicKey: string;\n  /** JWT access token (extracted from auth store — for direct API calls) */\n  accessToken: string;\n  /** Root IPNS name (extracted from vault store — for conflict detection API calls) */\n  rootIpnsName: string;\n}\n\n/**\n * Extract auth state from Zustand stores in the browser.\n *\n * After wallet login, Core Kit manages the secp256k1 identity in-browser.\n * This function extracts the public key, access token, and vault metadata\n * needed by tests that make direct API calls (conflict detection, sharing).\n */\nasync function extractAuthState(\n  page: Page\n): Promise<{ publicKey: string; accessToken: string; rootIpnsName: string }> {\n  return page.evaluate(() => {\n    const stores = (window as unknown as Record<string, unknown>).__ZUSTAND_STORES as {\n      auth: { getState: () => { accessToken: string; vaultKeypair?: { publicKey: Uint8Array } } };\n      vault: { getState: () => { rootIpnsName: string } };\n    };\n\n    const authState = stores.auth.getState();\n    const vaultState = stores.vault.getState();\n\n    // Convert Uint8Array public key to 0x-prefixed hex\n    const pubKeyBytes = authState.vaultKeypair?.publicKey;\n    let publicKey = '';\n    if (pubKeyBytes) {\n      const hex = Array.from(pubKeyBytes)\n        .map((b: number) => b.toString(16).padStart(2, '0'))\n        .join('');\n      publicKey = '0x' + hex;\n    }\n\n    return {\n      publicKey,\n      accessToken: authState.accessToken,\n      rootIpnsName: vaultState.rootIpnsName,\n    };\n  });\n}",
  "new_string": "/**\n * A fully authenticated test account using wallet-based login.\n * Each account has an isolated browser context, a random EVM identity,\n * and its public key extracted from the Settings page UI after login.\n */\nexport interface WalletTestAccount {\n  name: string;\n  context: BrowserContext;\n  page: Page;\n  account: PrivateKeyAccount;\n  /** 0x04-prefixed secp256k1 public key (for sharing — extracted from Settings page) */\n  publicKey: string;\n}\n\n/**\n * Extract the user's public key from the Settings page UI.\n *\n * Navigates to /settings, reads the key from the visible element,\n * then navigates back to /files. This avoids reading Zustand internals.\n */\nasync function extractPublicKeyFromUI(page: Page): Promise<string> {\n  // Navigate to settings\n  await page.evaluate(() => {\n    window.location.hash = '#/settings';\n  });\n  await page.waitForURL('**/settings', { timeout: 15_000 });\n\n  // Read public key from the Settings page\n  const pubKeyElement = page.locator('.settings-pubkey-value');\n  await pubKeyElement.waitFor({ state: 'visible', timeout: 10_000 });\n  const publicKey = (await pubKeyElement.textContent()) ?? '';\n\n  // Navigate back to files\n  await page.evaluate(() => {\n    window.location.hash = '#/files';\n  });\n  await page.waitForURL('**/files', { timeout: 15_000 });\n\n  return publicKey;\n}"
}

> AGENT

Done. PR #302 now has three commits:

1. **Expose stores unconditionally** (will be squashed away)
2. **Gate behind `VITE_E2E`** — `main.tsx` only exposes `__ZUSTAND_STORES` when `DEV` or `VITE_E2E` is set; CI workflow sets `VITE_E2E=true`
3. **Replace store access with UI checks**:
   - **Vault readiness** — `wallet-login-helpers` and test 3.7 now wait for file list / empty state instead of polling Zustand
   - **Public key extraction** — `multi-account-wallet` navigates to Settings page, reads `.settings-pubkey-value`, navigates back
   - **Removed** `accessToken` and `rootIpnsName` from `WalletTestAccount` (only conflict-detection needs those, and it reads stores directly)

> DEVELOPER

https://github.com/FSM1/cipher-box/pull/302#discussion_r2968678584 mentions a really nice approach, especially now that we only really need one value for one set of tests.

--- 2968678584 by coderabbitai ---
File: apps/web/src/main.tsx:None
_⚠️ Potential issue_ | _🟠 Major_

**Gate `__ZUSTAND_STORES` behind an E2E-only build flag.**

These are the raw Zustand hooks, so they expose `getState`/`setState`, not a read-only view. `tests/e2e/utils/web3auth-helpers.ts:230-268` already mutates them, and `apps/web/src/stores/auth.store.ts`, `vault.store.ts`, and `folder.store.ts` show the exposed state includes `accessToken`, `vaultKeypair.privateKey`, `rootFolderKey`, `rootIpnsKeypair.privateKey`, and decrypted folder keys. Making this unconditional turns that sensitive state into a stable `window` API that any script running in the page can read or mutate in real user sessions. Keep the bridge behind a dedicated E2E build flag, and ideally expose only the minimal test helpers Playwright needs instead of the full store objects.

<deta
```

> AGENT

`web3auth-helpers` is only imported by `multi-account.ts` (the old email-based flow, not the wallet-based one). None of the current active test specs import it directly. So `web3auth-helpers` is legacy code — the `__ZUSTAND_STORES` usage there doesn't matter for CI.

That means the **only active usage** that truly needs store access is `conflict-detection.spec.ts` — and it only reads `accessToken` and `rootIpnsName`. Instead of exposing the full Zustand stores, we can expose just a minimal `window.__E2E` helper:

Done. The PR now exposes **zero** Zustand store hooks to `window`. Instead:

- **`window.__E2E`** — only two getter functions (`getAccessToken()`, `getRootIpnsName()`), only in `VITE_E2E` builds, only used by conflict-detection
- **Public keys** — read from the Settings page UI
- **Vault readiness** — detected by file list / empty state appearing
- **Production builds** — no `__E2E`, no `__ZUSTAND_STORES`, nothing on `window`

--- Thread 1 (ID: PRRT_kwDOQ6DXvM512LFs) ---
Author: copilot-pull-request-reviewer
File: apps/web/src/main.tsx:None
Comment DB ID: 2968722423
Body: `import.meta.env.VITE_E2E` is a string env var in Vite; any non-empty value (including `'false'`) will be truthy and will expose `window.__ZUSTAND_STORES` in builds where it was intended to stay disabled. To keep the security gate reliable, compare explicitly (e.g. `import.meta.env.VITE_E2E === 'true'`) and consider aligning the comment with the fact that the exported store objects include mutators (e.g. `setState`) and sensitive in-memory values like `accessToken`.

--- Thread 2 (ID: PRRT_kwDOQ6DXvM512LF4) ---
Author: copilot-pull-request-reviewer
File: tests/e2e/utils/multi-account-wallet.ts:43
Comment DB ID: 2968722442
Body: `extractPublicKeyFromUI` returns `textContent()` as-is and falls back to `''` on null. If the selector changes, the element is empty, or whitespace is included, downstream tests will get an empty/invalid key and fail later with less actionable errors. Consider `trim()` + validating the expected format (e.g. non-empty and starts with `0x04`) and throw a descriptive error when it doesn’t match.

--- Thread 3 (ID: PRRT_kwDOQ6DXvM512LF9) ---
Author: copilot-pull-request-reviewer
File: tests/e2e/utils/multi-account-wallet.ts:86
Comment DB ID: 2968722454
Body: `createWalletTestAccount` waits for the file list/empty state after `loginViaWallet()`, but `loginViaWallet()` already performs the same readiness wait on success. This duplicates a potentially-30s wait and adds test runtime without increasing correctness; consider removing one of the waits (or centralizing the readiness guarantee in one place).
```

> AGENT

Let me triage and fix each:

**Thread 1**: Valid — `VITE_E2E === 'true'` is safer. But the comment references `__ZUSTAND_STORES` which we already replaced with `__E2E` in the latest commit.

**Thread 2**: Valid — should validate the extracted public key.

**Thread 3**: Valid — `loginViaWallet` already waits for file list, so `createWalletTestAccount` duplicates it.

> AGENT

The error is clear: `Cannot read properties of undefined (reading 'getAccessToken')` — `window.__E2E` is `undefined`. The `__E2E` object is set inside an async `import().then()`, but the conflict-detection test reads it in `beforeAll` right after login. The dynamic import hasn't resolved yet.

> AGENT

It's not imported statically. But `useAuthStore` is. The fix is simple — use a static import for vault store too, since Vite will tree-shake the entire `if` block (including the import) out of non-E2E builds anyway:

{
  "replace_all": false,

> AGENT

The `import('./stores/vault.store').then()` was async — `__E2E` wasn't set by the time the conflict-detection test's `beforeAll` ran `page.evaluate()`. Switched to a static import, which is synchronous. Vite will tree-shake the entire block (including the import) out of production builds where `VITE_E2E` isn't set.

> DEVELOPER

ok that did not work again. failed on the conflict resolution tests, getAccessToken. 

Would using 2 separate browser sessions logged in to the same user account be a rational substitute to the current zustand store fiddling?

> AGENT

Yes, that's a much cleaner approach. Two sessions with the same wallet identity is exactly how a real user would create a conflict (two devices, same account). It eliminates `__E2E`, `VITE_E2E`, and all the store access in one shot.

The flow would be:
1. **Session A** — logs in with wallet account X, uploads seed file
2. **Session B** — logs in with same wallet account X (same private key → same Core Kit identity → same vault)
3. **Session B** — uploads/mutates through the UI → bumps the server sequence
4. **Session A** — tries to upload → stale sequence → 409 conflict → auto-resolve

The only question is whether two concurrent Core Kit sessions with the same DKG identity on Sapphire Devnet would cause issues. Since each context initializes independently with the same wallet address → same userId → same TSS shares, it should work — they'd both derive the same keypair. It would add ~30-60s for the second login though.

Want me to rewrite the conflict-detection tests this way? That would let us remove the `VITE_E2E` flag, the `__E2E` window object, and the vault store import from `main.tsx` entirely — back to a completely clean production build with zero test scaffolding.

> AGENT

There it is — **`pnpm --filter @cipherbox/web dev`**. The E2E tests use Vite dev server, not the production build. The production build step is irrelevant — `VITE_E2E` needs to be in the **dev server's** environment, not the build step.

The `.env` file created for the web app doesn't include `VITE_E2E`:

```
echo "VITE_WEB3AUTH_CLIENT_ID=..." > apps/web/.env
echo "VITE_API_URL=http://localhost:3000" >> apps/web/.env
```

And in dev mode, `import.meta.env.DEV` is `true`, but we removed the `DEV` check in the last iteration. So `__E2E` is never set.

Given this — the dual-session approach you suggested is the right call. It eliminates the entire `__E2E` / `VITE_E2E` complexity. Want me to go ahead and rewrite the conflict-detection tests to use a second browser session?

  "new_string": "export function ensureFolderRegistered(folder: FolderNode): void {\n  const client = getSdkClient();\n\n  // Don't overwrite if SDK already has this folder — its internal state\n  // is authoritative (correct sequence number, keys, children from mutations)\n  if (client.hasFolder(folder.ipnsName)) return;\n\n  // Guard: don't register placeholder folders that haven't loaded yet —\n  // empty key material would cause crypto/IPNS errors on subsequent mutations\n  if (!folder.isLoaded || folder.folderKey.length === 0 || folder.ipnsPrivateKey.length === 0) {\n    return;\n  }\n\n  client.registerFolder(\n    folder.ipnsName,\n    folder.folderKey,\n    {\n      publicKey: new Uint8Array(0), // Public key derived from private key when needed\n      privateKey: folder.ipnsPrivateKey,\n    },\n    folder.children,\n    folder.sequenceNumber\n  );\n}"
